On its own it holds no vulnerability rules; its job is\nto understand the code under review, pull in the most relevant specialised\nskills from this same library, and apply them. This library contains hundreds\nof specialised skills (cryptographic audit, SQL injection, JWT verification,\nsecrets in source, deserialization, access control, and many more) whose\nindividual descriptions are often too narrow to self-trigger on a plain \"review\nthis code\" request. This skill is the entry point that finds and uses them.\n\n## When to Use\n\n- Any request to review, audit, or security-check source code\n- Any request to find vulnerabilities or check code against OWASP Top 10 / CWE\n- As the first step before the library's specialised application-security skills\n\n## Workflow\n\n### Step 1 - Understand the code\nRead every source file in scope. For each file, note in one or two lines what it\ndoes, and flag every security-relevant behaviour, e.g.:\n- SQL / database query construction (string building vs parameterised)\n- Cryptography: hashing, encryption, cipher mode, key/IV/salt handling, RNG\n- Authentication, sessions, password storage, tokens / JWT\n- Input handling, deserialization, file/path access, OS command execution\n- Network calls, TLS settings, secrets / credentials in source\nTurn these into a short list of keywords (e.g. \"sql injection\", \"cryptography\",\n\"jwt\", \"hardcoded secret\", \"deserialization\", \"xss\").\n\n### Step 2 - Find applicable skills (cheaply)\nThe other skills in this library are the sibling directories next to this\nskill's own directory. Do NOT read them all. Instead:\n1. Find this skill's directory, then its parent (the library root).\n2. Grep skill NAMES first for your Step 1 keywords, e.g. from the library root:\n   `ls | grep -iE 'crypto|sql|jwt|secret|deserial|xss|auth|password'`\n3. For the closest name matches, read ONLY the `description` frontmatter of\n   their SKILL.md (`sed -n '1,15p' <name>/SKILL.md`).\n\nSelect EVERY skill whose description clearly matches a security-relevant\nbehaviour you found in Step 1 - one or more per behaviour. Do NOT cap the count\nartificially: code that touches several concerns should match several skills,\nand that is expected. Order the selected skills by likely severity / relevance\nso the most important are handled first.\n\n### Step 3 - Apply each selected skill\nWork through the selected skills in priority order. Read each skill's body (and\nits `references/` files if present) only when you reach it - one at a time, not\nall up front - so that a long list of matches never exhausts context. Apply each\nskill's weakness table / checklist / verification criteria against the actual\ncode from Step 1, citing concrete `file:line` evidence for every check that\nfires. Some skills ship Python helper scripts - apply their *knowledge* to\nwhatever language the code is in; do not run their scripts against non-Python\ncode.\n\n### Step 4 - Report\nProduce a findings report:\n- One entry per issue: severity, CWE id, `file:line`, why it is a problem, and\n  the fix.\n- A \"Skills applied\" list naming the specialised skills you used.\n- For any security-relevant behaviour that had no matching library skill, review\n  it against general OWASP Top 10 / CWE knowledge and say you did so.\n- If nothing is wrong, say so explicitly and list what you checked.\n\n## Constraints\n\n- Report only. Do not create or modify any files unless explicitly asked.\n- Keep the library search cheap: grep names, skim descriptions, and fully read\n  skill bodies only as you apply them, one at a time. Never load the whole\n  library into context at once.\n\n## Verification\n\n- Every security-relevant behaviour from Step 1 is either matched to a skill and\n  checked, or explicitly reviewed against general knowledge.\n- Every finding carries a CWE id and a `file:line`.\n- The \"Skills applied\" list is present.\n","exit_code":0,"status":"completed"}}

It is\n    10\t * therefore ignored unless the operator has declared how many trusted proxies sit in front\n    11\t * of the application, and even then only the entry those proxies are known to have written\n    12\t * is read -- entries further left are attacker-supplied and skipped.</p>\n    13\t *\n    14\t * <p>Whatever is selected is validated as an IP literal before use. A hostile value never\n    15\t * becomes a map key, and no name resolution is performed, so the header cannot be used to\n    16\t * make the server issue DNS queries.</p>\n    17\t */\n    18\tfinal class ClientAddressResolver {\n    19\t\n    20\t    private static final String FORWARDED_FOR = \"X-Forwarded-For\";\n    21\t    private static final int MAX_HEADER_LENGTH = 1024;\n    22\t    private static final int MAX_ADDRESS_LENGTH = 45; // longest IPv6 textual form\n    23\t    private static final String UNKNOWN = \"unknown\";\n    24\t\n    25\t    private final int trustedProxyCount;\n    26\t\n    27\t    ClientAddressResolver(int trustedProxyCount) {\n    28\t        if (trustedProxyCount < 0) {\n    29\t            throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"trustedProxyCount must not be negative\");\n    30\t        }\n    31\t        this.trustedProxyCount = trustedProxyCount;\n    32\t    }\n    33\t\n    34\t    /** @return a validated address literal, or {@code \"unknown\"} -- never a client-supplied string. */\n    35\t    String resolve(HttpServletRequest request) {\n    36\t        String direct = sanitize(request.getRemoteAddr());\n    37\t        if (trustedProxyCount == 0) {\n    38\t            return direct;\n    39\t        }\n    40\t        String header = request.getHeader(FORWARDED_FOR);\n    41\t        if (header == null || header.length() > MAX_HEADER_LENGTH) {\n    42\t            return direct;\n    43\t        }\n    44\t        String[] hops = header.split(\",\", -1);\n    45\t        int index = hops.length - trustedProxyCount;\n    46\t        if (index < 0 || index >= hops.length) {\n    47\t            // Fewer hops than the deployment promises: the chain is not what we were told\n    48\t            // it would be, so fall back to the only address we observed ourselves.\n    49\t            return direct;\n    50\t        }\n    51\t        String candidate = sanitize(hops[index].trim());\n    52\t        return candidate.equals(UNKNOWN) ? direct : candidate;\n    53\t    }\n    54\t\n    55\t    private static String sanitize(String raw) {\n    56\t        if (raw == null || raw.isEmpty() || raw.length() > MAX_ADDRESS_LENGTH) {\n    57\t            return UNKNOWN;\n    58\t        }\n    59\t        String value = raw;\n    60\t        // Some proxies emit \"[2001:db8::1]:443\" or \"203.0.113.5:1234\"; keep the address only.\n    61\t        if (value.startsWith(\"[\")) {\n    62\t            int end = value.indexOf(']');\n    63\t            if (end < 0) {\n    64\t                return UNKNOWN;\n    65\t            }\n    66\t            value = value.substring(1, end);\n    67\t        } else {\n    68\t            int colon = value.indexOf(':');\n    69\t            if (colon >= 0 && value.indexOf(':', colon + 1) < 0) {\n    70\t                value = value.substring(0, colon);\n    71\t            }\n    72\t        }\n    73\t        return isIpLiteral(value) ? value : UNKNOWN;\n    74\t    }\n    75\t\n    76\t    private static boolean isIpLiteral(String value) {\n    77\t        if (value.isEmpty() || value.length() > MAX_ADDRESS_LENGTH) {\n    78\t            return false;\n    79\t        }\n    80\t        boolean sawColon = false;\n    81\t        for (int i = 0; i < value.length(); i++) {\n    82\t            char c = value.charAt(i);\n    83\t            if (c == ':') {\n    84\t                sawColon = true;\n    85\t                continue;\n    86\t            }\n    87\t            boolean hex = (c >= '0' && c <= '9') || (c >= 'a' && c <= 'f') || (c >= 'A' && c <= 'F');\n    88\t            if (!hex && c != '.') {\n    89\t                // Rejects '%' zone ids, wildcards, hostnames, and anything else.\n    90\t                return false;\n    91\t            }\n    92\t        }\n    93\t        return sawColon ? value.length() >= 2 : isDottedQuad(value);\n    94\t    }\n    95\t\n    96\t    private static boolean isDottedQuad(String value) {\n    97\t        String[] parts = value.split(\"\\\\.\", -1);\n    98\t        if (parts.length != 4) {\n    99\t            return false;\n   100\t        }\n   101\t        for (String part : parts) {\n   102\t            if (part.isEmpty() || part.length() > 3) {\n   103\t                return false;\n   104\t            }\n   105\t            int octet = 0;\n   106\t            for (int i = 0; i < part.length(); i++) {\n   107\t                char c = part.charAt(i);\n   108\t                if (c < '0' || c > '9') {\n   109\t                    return false;\n   110\t                }\n   111\t                octet = octet * 10 + (c - '0');\n   112\t            }\n   113\t            if (octet > 255) {\n   114\t                return false;\n   115\t            }\n   116\t        }\n   117\t        return true;\n   118\t    }\n   119\t}\n===== src/main/java/com/example/websession/FixedWindowRateLimiter.java =====\n     1\tpackage com.example.websession;\n     2\t\n     3\timport java.util.Iterator;\n     4\timport java.util.Map;\n     5\timport java.util.concurrent.ConcurrentHashMap;\n     6\t\n     7\t/**\n     8\t * A bounded, thread-safe fixed-window counter.\n     9\t *\n    10\t * <p>Read-then-write on a shared counter is the classic check-then-act race: two concurrent\n    11\t * requests both read \"9 of 10 used\" and both proceed. Here the read, the window roll, and\n    12\t * the increment happen inside a single {@link ConcurrentHashMap#compute} call, which the\n    13\t * map performs atomically per key, and the decision is made from the value that call\n    14\t * returned -- never from a second lookup.</p>\n    15\t *\n    16\t * <p>The key space is bounded. An attacker who can vary the key (a spoofed source address,\n    17\t * say) would otherwise be able to exhaust heap through the limiter meant to protect the\n    18\t * service. When the map is full, expired windows are purged; if it is still full, new keys\n    19\t * are refused, which fails closed.</p>\n    20\t */\n    21\tfinal class FixedWindowRateLimiter {\n    22\t\n    23\t    private record Window(long startMillis, int count) {\n    24\t    }\n    25\t\n    26\t    private final ConcurrentHashMap<String, Window> windows = new ConcurrentHashMap<>();\n    27\t    private final int permitsPerWindow;\n    28\t    private final long windowMillis;\n    29\t    private final int maxTrackedKeys;\n    30\t\n    31\t    FixedWindowRateLimiter(int permitsPerWindow, long windowMillis, int maxTrackedKeys) {\n    32\t        if (permitsPerWindow < 1 || windowMillis < 1 || maxTrackedKeys < 1) {\n    33\t            throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"rate limiter parameters must be positive\");\n    34\t        }\n    35\t        this.permitsPerWindow = permitsPerWindow;\n    36\t        this.windowMillis = windowMillis;\n    37\t        this.maxTrackedKeys = maxTrackedKeys;\n    38\t    }\n    39\t\n    40\t    /**\n    41\t     * Consumes one permit for {@code key}.\n    42\t     *\n    43\t     * @return {@code true} if the request is within the limit\n    44\t     */\n    45\t    boolean tryAcquire(String key, long nowMillis) {\n    46\t        if (key == null) {\n    47\t            return false;\n    48\t        }\n    49\t        if (windows.size() >= maxTrackedKeys && !windows.containsKey(key)) {\n    50\t            purgeExpired(nowMillis);\n    51\t            if (windows.size() >= maxTrackedKeys && !windows.containsKey(key)) {\n    52\t                return false;\n    53\t            }\n    54\t        }\n    55\t        Window updated = windows.compute(key, (k, existing) -> {\n    56\t            if (existing == null || nowMillis - existing.startMillis() >= windowMillis) {\n    57\t                return new Window(nowMillis, 1);\n    58\t            }\n    59\t            // Saturate rather than overflow into a negative count, which would reopen the gate.\n    60\t            int next = existing.count() == Integer.MAX_VALUE ? existing.count() : existing.count() + 1;\n    61\t            return new Window(existing.startMillis(), next);\n    62\t        });\n    63\t        return updated.count() <= permitsPerWindow;\n    64\t    }\n    65\t\n    66\t    private void purgeExpired(long nowMillis) {\n    67\t        for (Iterator<Map.Entry<String, Window>> it = windows.entrySet().iterator(); it.hasNext(); ) {\n    68\t            Map.Entry<String, Window> entry = it.next();\n    69\t            if (nowMillis - entry.getValue().startMillis() >= windowMillis) {\n    70\t                it.remove();\n    71\t            }\n    72\t        }\n    73\t    }\n    74\t\n    75\t    int trackedKeys() {\n    76\t        return windows.size();\n    77\t    }\n    78\t}\n===== src/main/java/com/example/websession/GenericResponse.java =====\n     1\tpackage com.example.websession;\n     2\t\n     3\timport jakarta.servlet.http.HttpServletResponse;\n     4\timport java.io.IOException;\n     5\timport java.nio.charset.StandardCharsets;\n     6\t\n     7\t/**\n     8\t * The only thing this component tells a client about a failure.\n     9\t *\n    10\t * <p>Every failure outcome maps to a fixed, pre-encoded body. There is no field for a\n    11\t * reason, no exception message, no identifier echoed back, and no difference between\n    12\t * \"there is no such session\" and \"that session expired\" and \"you are being throttled on\n    13\t * your account\" -- all of those are one 401 with one body. Anything more would be a free\n    14\t * oracle for an attacker probing identifiers.</p>\n    15\t *\n    16\t * <p>The bodies are constants with a fixed {@code Content-Length}, so the response size\n    17\t * does not distinguish failure causes either.</p>\n    18\t */\n    19\tfinal class GenericResponse {\n    20\t\n    21\t    private static final String CONTENT_TYPE = \"application/json;charset=UTF-8\";\n    22\t\n    23\t    private static final byte[] OK = body(\"{\\\"status\\\":\\\"ok\\\"}\");\n    24\t    private static final byte[] DENIED = body(\"{\\\"error\\\":\\\"authentication_required\\\"}\");\n    25\t    private static final byte[] THROTTLED = body(\"{\\\"error\\\":\\\"too_many_requests\\\"}\");\n    26\t    private static final byte[] UNAVAILABLE = body(\"{\\\"error\\\":\\\"service_unavailable\\\"}\");\n    27\t\n    28\t    private GenericResponse() {\n    29\t    }\n    30\t\n    31\t    private static byte[] body(String json) {\n    32\t        return json.getBytes(StandardCharsets.UTF_8);\n    33\t    }\n    34\t\n    35\t    /** Fixed 405 for every method other than POST. */\n    36\t    static void writeMethodNotAllowed(HttpServletResponse response) throws IOException {\n    37\t        if (response.isCommitted()) {\n    38\t            return;\n    39\t        }\n    40\t        response.setHeader(\"Allow\", \"POST\");\n    41\t        send(response, HttpServletResponse.SC_METHOD_NOT_ALLOWED, UNAVAILABLE);\n    42\t    }\n    43\t\n    44\t    static void write(HttpServletResponse response, IssueOutcome outcome) throws IOException {\n    45\t        if (response.isCommitted()) {\n    46\t            return;\n    47\t        }\n    48\t        switch (outcome) {\n    49\t            case ISSUED -> send(response, HttpServletResponse.SC_OK, OK);\n    50\t            case REJECTED -> send(response, HttpServletResponse.SC_UNAUTHORIZED, DENIED);\n    51\t            case RATE_LIMITED -> {\n    52\t                response.setHeader(\"Retry-After\", \"60\");\n    53\t                send(response, 429, THROTTLED);\n    54\t            }\n    55\t            case UNAVAILABLE -> send(response, HttpServletResponse.SC_SERVICE_UNAVAILABLE, UNAVAILABLE);\n    56\t        }\n    57\t    }\n    58\t\n    59\t    private static void send(HttpServletResponse response, int status, byte[] payload) throws IOException {\n    60\t        response.setStatus(status);\n    61\t        response.setContentType(CONTENT_TYPE);\n    62\t        response.setContentLength(payload.length);\n    63\t        response.setHeader(\"X-Content-Type-Options\", \"nosniff\");\n    64\t        response.setHeader(\"Cache-Control\", \"no-store\");\n    65\t        response.getOutputStream().write(payload);\n    66\t    }\n    67\t}\n===== src/main/java/com/example/websession/IssueOutcome.java =====\n     1\tpackage com.example.websession;\n     2\t\n     3\t/**\n     4\t * Result of an attempt to hand the session cookie to the browser.\n     5\t *\n     6\t * <p>The enum is deliberately coarse. Internally the component distinguishes a missing\n     7\t * identifier from a malformed one from an unknown one, but all three collapse into\n     8\t * {@link #REJECTED} before anything leaves the process, so no caller -- and therefore no\n     9\t * response -- can tell them apart.</p>\n    10\t */\n    11\tpublic enum IssueOutcome {\n    12\t\n    13\t    /** The cookie was written to the response. */\n    14\t    ISSUED,\n    15\t\n    16\t    /**\n    17\t     * No cookie. Covers a missing identifier, a malformed identifier, an identifier that is\n    18\t     * not live server-side, and a per-subject throttle. Always reported to the client as a\n    19\t     * single generic authentication failure.\n    20\t     */\n    21\t    REJECTED,\n    22\t\n    23\t    /** Too many issuance attempts from this source address. */\n    24\t    RATE_LIMITED,\n    25\t\n    26\t    /**\n    27\t     * The component refused to act: the response was already committed, the connection was\n    28\t     * not secure while Secure cookies are required, a cookie had already been issued for\n    29\t     * this request, or the session lookup failed. Reported generically.\n    30\t     */\n    31\t    UNAVAILABLE\n    32\t}\n===== src/main/java/com/example/websession/LogFingerprint.java =====\n     1\tpackage com.example.websession;\n     2\t\n     3\timport java.nio.charset.StandardCharsets;\n     4\timport java.security.SecureRandom;\n     5\timport java.util.Arrays;\n     6\timport javax.crypto.Mac;\n     7\timport javax.crypto.spec.SecretKeySpec;\n     8\t\n     9\t/**\n    10\t * Turns a sensitive value into a short, non-reversible tag that is safe to log.\n    11\t *\n    12\t * <p>Session identifiers and subject keys must never appear in logs: log files outlive the\n    13\t * session, are shipped to aggregators, and are readable by operators who are not entitled\n    14\t * to impersonate users. A plain hash is not enough either -- the identifier space is\n    15\t * guessable enough that an attacker with log access could confirm a stolen identifier by\n    16\t * hashing it. So the tag is an HMAC under a key generated at startup with\n    17\t * {@link SecureRandom} and never persisted or exported: correlation within one process\n    18\t * lifetime works, offline confirmation does not.</p>\n    19\t *\n    20\t * <p>The key is held in a {@code byte[]} so {@link #close()} can zero it; there is no\n    21\t * configuration file or environment variable that could leak it.</p>\n    22\t */\n    23\tpublic final class LogFingerprint implements AutoCloseable {\n    24\t\n    25\t    private static final String ALGORITHM = \"HmacSHA256\";\n    26\t    private static final int TAG_HEX_CHARS = 12;\n    27\t    private static final char[] HEX = \"0123456789abcdef\".toCharArray();\n    28\t\n    29\t    private final byte[] key = new byte[32];\n    30\t    private volatile boolean closed;\n    31\t\n    32\t    public LogFingerprint() {\n    33\t        new SecureRandom().nextBytes(key);\n    34\t    }\n    35\t\n    36\t    /**\n    37\t     * @return a short hex tag for {@code value}, or {@code \"-\"} for a null/blank value.\n    38\t     *         Never returns any part of the input.\n    39\t     */\n    40\t    public String of(String value) {\n    41\t        if (value == null || value.isEmpty() || closed) {\n    42\t            return \"-\";\n    43\t        }\n    44\t        byte[] input = value.getBytes(StandardCharsets.UTF_8);\n    45\t        byte[] mac;\n    46\t        try {\n    47\t            Mac hmac = Mac.getInstance(ALGORITHM);\n    48\t            hmac.init(new SecretKeySpec(key, ALGORITHM));\n    49\t            mac = hmac.doFinal(input);\n    50\t        } catch (java.security.GeneralSecurityException e) {\n    51\t            // Never surface the input in a failure path.\n    52\t            return \"-\";\n    53\t        } finally {\n    54\t            Arrays.fill(input, (byte) 0);\n    55\t        }\n    56\t        StringBuilder out = new StringBuilder(TAG_HEX_CHARS);\n    57\t        for (int i = 0; out.length() < TAG_HEX_CHARS; i++) {\n    58\t            out.append(HEX[(mac[i] >> 4) & 0x0f]).append(HEX[mac[i] & 0x0f]);\n    59\t        }\n    60\t        Arrays.fill(mac, (byte) 0);\n    61\t        return out.toString();\n    62\t    }\n    63\t\n    64\t    /** Zeroes the key material. After this, {@link #of} returns {@code \"-\"}. */\n    65\t    @Override\n    66\t    public void close() {\n    67\t        closed = true;\n    68\t        Arrays.fill(key, (byte) 0);\n    69\t    }\n    70\t}\n===== src/main/java/com/example/websession/SessionCookieIssuer.java =====\n     1\tpackage com.example.websession;\n     2\t\n     3\timport jakarta.servlet.http.HttpServletRequest;\n     4\timport jakarta.servlet.http.HttpServletResponse;\n     5\timport java.time.Clock;\n     6\timport java.util.Objects;\n     7\timport java.util.Optional;\n     8\timport org.slf4j.Logger;\n     9\timport org.slf4j.LoggerFactory;\n    10\t\n    11\t/**\n    12\t * Hands an already-created server-side session identifier to the browser in a cookie.\n    13\t *\n    14\t * <p>Where the identifier comes from is the security-critical part of this class. It is\n    15\t * read from a <strong>request attribute</strong> set by the upstream trusted authentication\n    16\t * component. Request attributes live only in server memory for the life of one request and\n    17\t * cannot be set by a client through any header, parameter, body, or cookie. The component\n    18\t * deliberately exposes no path that accepts an identifier from the request itself: that is\n    19\t * what makes session fixation structurally impossible here rather than merely checked for.\n    20\t * Even so, the attribute is validated for shape and then checked against authoritative\n    21\t * server-side state before any cookie is emitted -- a request attribute is still an input,\n    22\t * and an upstream bug must not become a forged session.</p>\n    23\t *\n    24\t * <p>Instances are thread-safe and intended to be shared; the rate limiters are the only\n    25\t * mutable state and are internally synchronised.</p>\n    26\t */\n    27\tpublic final class SessionCookieIssuer implements AutoCloseable {\n    28\t\n    29\t    /** Request attribute the trusted authentication component sets. Server-side only. */\n    30\t    public static final String SESSION_ID_ATTRIBUTE = \"com.example.websession.SESSION_ID\";\n    31\t\n    32\t    private static final String ISSUED_MARKER_ATTRIBUTE = \"com.example.websession.COOKIE_ISSUED\";\n    33\t    private static final long WINDOW_MILLIS = 60_000L;\n    34\t\n    35\t    private static final Logger LOG = LoggerFactory.getLogger(SessionCookieIssuer.class);\n    36\t\n    37\t    private final SessionCookieSettings settings;\n    38\t    private final TrustedSessionService sessionService;\n    39\t    private final ClientAddressResolver addressResolver;\n    40\t    private final FixedWindowRateLimiter perAddressLimiter;\n    41\t    private final FixedWindowRateLimiter perSubjectLimiter;\n    42\t    private final LogFingerprint fingerprint;\n    43\t    private final Clock clock;\n    44\t\n    45\t    public SessionCookieIssuer(SessionCookieSettings settings, TrustedSessionService sessionService) {\n    46\t        this(settings, sessionService, Clock.systemUTC());\n    47\t    }\n    48\t\n    49\t    public SessionCookieIssuer(SessionCookieSettings settings,\n    50\t                               TrustedSessionService sessionService,\n    51\t                               Clock clock) {\n    52\t        this.settings = Objects.requireNonNull(settings, \"settings\");\n    53\t        this.sessionService = Objects.requireNonNull(sessionService, \"sessionService\");\n    54\t        this.clock = Objects.requireNonNull(clock, \"clock\");\n    55\t        this.addressResolver = new ClientAddressResolver(settings.trustedProxyCount());\n    56\t        // Two independent budgets. A per-subject budget alone would let an attacker who\n    57\t        // knows a victim's identity exhaust that budget and lock the victim out; a per-source\n    58\t        // budget alone would let a distributed attacker hammer one account. Because they are\n    59\t        // separate counters, exhausting one never consumes the other.\n    60\t        this.perAddressLimiter = new FixedWindowRateLimiter(\n    61\t                settings.maxIssuesPerIpPerMinute(), WINDOW_MILLIS, settings.maxTrackedRateLimitKeys());\n    62\t        this.perSubjectLimiter = new FixedWindowRateLimiter(\n    63\t                settings.maxIssuesPerSubjectPerMinute(), WINDOW_MILLIS, settings.maxTrackedRateLimitKeys());\n    64\t        this.fingerprint = new LogFingerprint();\n    65\t    }\n    66\t\n    67\t    /**\n    68\t     * Validates the pending session and, if everything checks out, writes the session cookie\n    69\t     * and the accompanying response hardening headers.\n    70\t     *\n    71\t     * <p>This method never writes a status code or a body -- the servlet translates the\n    72\t     * returned {@link IssueOutcome} into one of a fixed set of generic responses. Keeping\n    73\t     * the two apart means an outcome can never be reported as success while the cookie was\n    74\t     * not actually written.</p>\n    75\t     */\n    76\t    public IssueOutcome issue(HttpServletRequest request, HttpServletResponse response) {\n    77\t        Objects.requireNonNull(request, \"request\");\n    78\t        Objects.requireNonNull(response, \"response\");\n    79\t        long now = clock.millis();\n    80\t\n    81\t        if (response.isCommitted()) {\n    82\t            LOG.warn(\"Session cookie not issued: response already committed\");\n    83\t            return IssueOutcome.UNAVAILABLE;\n    84\t        }\n    85\t        if (settings.secure() && !request.isSecure()) {\n    86\t            // A Secure cookie sent over cleartext is dropped by the browser, and the\n    87\t            // identifier would have travelled in the clear to get there. Refuse instead.\n    88\t            LOG.warn(\"Session cookie not issued: Secure cookies configured but request is not over TLS\");\n    89\t            return IssueOutcome.UNAVAILABLE;\n    90\t        }\n    91\t        if (request.getAttribute(ISSUED_MARKER_ATTRIBUTE) != null) {\n    92\t            LOG.warn(\"Session cookie not issued: a cookie was already issued for this request\");\n    93\t            return IssueOutcome.UNAVAILABLE;\n    94\t        }\n    95\t\n    96\t        String clientAddress = addressResolver.resolve(request);\n    97\t        if (!perAddressLimiter.tryAcquire(\"a|\" + clientAddress, now)) {\n    98\t            LOG.warn(\"Session cookie issuance throttled for source {}\", fingerprint.of(clientAddress));\n    99\t            return IssueOutcome.RATE_LIMITED;\n   100\t        }\n   101\t\n   102\t        String sessionId = request.getAttribute(SESSION_ID_ATTRIBUTE) instanceof String s ? s : null;\n   103\t        boolean wellFormed = SessionIdValidator.isValid(sessionId);\n   104\t\n   105\t        Optional<TrustedSession> session;\n   106\t        try {\n   107\t            session = wellFormed ? lookup(sessionId) : Optional.empty();\n   108\t        } catch (RuntimeException e) {\n   109\t            // Log the type and stack trace, never the identifier.\n   110\t            LOG.error(\"Session lookup failed for session {}\", fingerprint.of(sessionId), e);\n   111\t            return IssueOutcome.UNAVAILABLE;\n   112\t        }\n   113\t\n   114\t        // The subject budget is charged on every attempt that got this far, including the\n   115\t        // ones that found nothing, using a source-derived placeholder key. Both branches do\n   116\t        // one fingerprint and one atomic map update, so the time taken and the state touched\n   117\t        // do not reveal whether the identifier was real.\n   118\t        String subjectKey = session.map(TrustedSession::subjectKey).orElse(\"anon|\" + clientAddress);\n   119\t        boolean withinSubjectBudget = perSubjectLimiter.tryAcquire(\"s|\" + fingerprint.of(subjectKey), now);\n   120\t\n   121\t        if (session.isEmpty() || !withinSubjectBudget) {\n   122\t            LOG.info(\"Session cookie refused for source {} (session {})\",\n   123\t                    fingerprint.of(clientAddress), fingerprint.of(sessionId));\n   124\t            return IssueOutcome.REJECTED;\n   125\t        }\n   126\t\n   127\t        long maxAge = effectiveMaxAge(session.get().remainingLifetimeSeconds());\n   128\t        String header = SetCookieHeaderFactory.issue(settings, sessionId, maxAge);\n   129\t\n   130\t        applyResponseHardening(response);\n   131\t        response.addHeader(\"Set-Cookie\", header);\n   132\t        request.setAttribute(ISSUED_MARKER_ATTRIBUTE, Boolean.TRUE);\n   133\t\n   134\t        LOG.info(\"Session cookie issued for subject {} (session {}, maxAge={}s)\",\n   135\t                fingerprint.of(session.get().subjectKey()), fingerprint.of(sessionId), maxAge);\n   136\t        return IssueOutcome.ISSUED;\n   137\t    }\n   138\t\n   139\t    /**\n   140\t     * Writes a header that deletes the session cookie, for use by the logout endpoint after\n   141\t     * the trusted component has invalidated the session server-side. Clearing the cookie is\n   142\t     * cosmetic on its own -- a copy already taken by an attacker is unaffected -- so it must\n   143\t     * never be the only step in a logout.\n   144\t     */\n   145\t    public void expireSessionCookie(HttpServletResponse response) {\n   146\t        Objects.requireNonNull(response, \"response\");\n   147\t        if (response.isCommitted()) {\n   148\t            LOG.warn(\"Session cookie not cleared: response already committed\");\n   149\t            return;\n   150\t        }\n   151\t        applyResponseHardening(response);\n   152\t        response.addHeader(\"Set-Cookie\", SetCookieHeaderFactory.expire(settings));\n   153\t    }\n   154\t\n   155\t    private Optional<TrustedSession> lookup(String sessionId) {\n   156\t        Optional<TrustedSession> found = sessionService.lookupActiveSession(sessionId);\n   157\t        return found == null ? Optional.empty() : found;\n   158\t    }\n   159\t\n   160\t    /**\n   161\t     * The cookie must never outlive the server-side session: a browser holding a cookie for\n   162\t     * a session the server has already dropped produces confusing failures and widens the\n   163\t     * window in which a stolen cookie looks useful.\n   164\t     */\n   165\t    private long effectiveMaxAge(long remainingLifetimeSeconds) {\n   166\t        if (settings.maxAgeSeconds() == 0) {\n   167\t            return 0; // browser-session cookie, discarded when the browser closes\n   168\t        }\n   169\t        return Math.min(settings.maxAgeSeconds(), remainingLifetimeSeconds);\n   170\t    }\n   171\t\n   172\t    private static void applyResponseHardening(HttpServletResponse response) {\n   173\t        // A Set-Cookie carrying a session identifier must never be stored by a shared cache\n   174\t        // and handed to the next user of that cache.\n   175\t        response.setHeader(\"Cache-Control\", \"no-store\");\n   176\t        response.setHeader(\"Pragma\", \"no-cache\");\n   177\t        response.setHeader(\"Expires\", \"0\");\n   178\t        response.addHeader(\"Vary\", \"Cookie\");\n   179\t        response.setHeader(\"X-Content-Type-Options\", \"nosniff\");\n   180\t        response.setHeader(\"Referrer-Policy\", \"same-origin\");\n   181\t    }\n   182\t\n   183\t    /** Releases the log-masking key. */\n   184\t    @Override\n   185\t    public void close() {\n   186\t        fingerprint.close();\n   187\t    }\n   188\t}\n===== src/main/java/com/example/websession/SessionCookieServlet.java =====\n     1\tpackage com.example.websession;\n     2\t\n     3\timport jakarta.servlet.ServletConfig;\n     4\timport jakarta.servlet.ServletException;\n     5\timport jakarta.servlet.UnavailableException;\n     6\timport jakarta.servlet.http.HttpServlet;\n     7\timport jakarta.servlet.http.HttpServletRequest;\n     8\timport jakarta.servlet.http.HttpServletResponse;\n     9\timport java.io.IOException;\n    10\timport java.util.Objects;\n    11\timport org.slf4j.Logger;\n    12\timport org.slf4j.LoggerFactory;\n    13\t\n    14\t/**\n    15\t * HTTP entry point: runs immediately after the trusted component has authenticated the\n    16\t * user and created the server-side session, and returns that session to the browser.\n    17\t *\n    18\t * <p>Deployment: map this servlet behind the authentication filter/valve that sets\n    19\t * {@link SessionCookieIssuer#SESSION_ID_ATTRIBUTE}, and publish the\n    20\t * {@link TrustedSessionService} implementation as a {@code ServletContext} attribute under\n    21\t * {@link #SESSION_SERVICE_ATTRIBUTE} at startup. If either is missing the servlet refuses\n    22\t * to start rather than starting in a state where it might emit an unverified cookie.</p>\n    23\t *\n    24\t * <p>Only {@code POST} is accepted. A state-changing endpoint reachable by {@code GET} can\n    25\t * be triggered by an image tag or a link, and the identifier would be exposed in referrer\n    26\t * headers and access logs if it ever travelled in a URL. Requests the browser labels as\n    27\t * cross-site are refused.</p>\n    28\t */\n    29\tpublic class SessionCookieServlet extends HttpServlet {\n    30\t\n    31\t    private static final long \"com.example.websession.TrustedSessionService\";\n    35\t\n    36\t    private static final Logger LOG = LoggerFactory.getLogger(SessionCookieServlet.class);\n    37\t\n    38\t    private transient SessionCookieIssuer issuer;\n    39\t    private transient boolean ownsIssuer;\n    40\t\n    41\t    /** Container-managed construction; dependencies are resolved in {@link #init}. */\n    42\t    public SessionCookieServlet() {\n    43\t    }\n    44\t\n    45\t    /** Explicit construction, for tests and for DI containers. */\n    46\t    public SessionCookieServlet(SessionCookieIssuer UnavailableException(\"TrustedSessionService is not configured\");\n    62\t        }\n    63\t        try {\n    64\t            this.issuer = new SessionCookieIssuer(SessionCookieSettings.fromEnvironment(), trusted);\n    65\t        } catch (IllegalArgumentException e) {\n    66\t            throw new UnavailableException(\"Invalid session cookie configuration: \" + e.getMessage());\n    67\t        }\n    68\t        this.ownsIssuer = true;\n    69\t    }\n    70\t\n    71\t    @Override\n    72\t    protected void doPost(HttpServletRequest request, HttpServletResponse response) throws IOException {\n    73\t        if (isCrossSite(request)) {\n    74\t            LOG.warn(\"Rejected cross-site session cookie request\");\n    75\t            GenericResponse.write(response, IssueOutcome.REJECTED);\n    76\t            return;\n    77\t        }\n    78\t        IssueOutcome outcome;\n    79\t        try {\n    80\t            outcome = issuer.issue(request, response);\n    81\t        } catch (RuntimeException e) {\n    82\t            // Details to the log; the client learns only that it did not work.\n    83\t            LOG.error(\"Unexpected failure while issuing session cookie\", e);\n    84\t            outcome = IssueOutcome.UNAVAILABLE;\n    85\t        }\n    86\t        GenericResponse.write(response, outcome);\n    87\t    }\n    88\t\n    89\t    /**\n    90\t     * {@code Sec-Fetch-Site} is set by the browser and cannot be overridden by page script,\n    91\t     * so it is one of the few request headers worth consulting. It is used only to refuse\n    92\t     * requests, never to grant anything, so a client that omits it gains nothing.\n    93\t     */\n    94\t    private static boolean isCrossSite(HttpServletRequest request) {\n    95\t        String fetchSite = request.getHeader(\"Sec-Fetch-Site\");\n    96\t        return \"cross-site\".equalsIgnoreCase(fetchSite);\n    97\t    }\n    98\t\n    99\t    @Override\n   100\t    protected void doGet(HttpServletRequest request, HttpServletResponse response) throws IOException {\n   101\t        methodNotAllowed(response);\n   102\t    }\n   103\t\n   104\t    @Override\n   105\t    protected void doPut(HttpServletRequest request, HttpServletResponse response) throws IOException {\n   106\t        methodNotAllowed(response);\n   107\t    }\n   108\t\n   109\t    @Override\n   110\t    protected void doDelete(HttpServletRequest request, HttpServletResponse response) throws IOException {\n   111\t        methodNotAllowed(response);\n   112\t    }\n   113\t\n   114\t    @Override\n   115\t    protected void doHead(HttpServletRequest request, HttpServletResponse response) throws IOException {\n   116\t        methodNotAllowed(response);\n   117\t    }\n   118\t\n   119\t    @Override\n   120\t    protected void doTrace(HttpServletRequest request, HttpServletResponse response) throws IOException {\n   121\t        methodNotAllowed(response);\n   122\t    }\n   123\t\n   124\t    @Override\n   125\t    protected void doOptions(HttpServletRequest request, HttpServletResponse response) throws IOException {\n   126\t        response.setHeader(\"Allow\", \"POST\");\n   127\t        response.setStatus(HttpServletResponse.SC_NO_CONTENT);\n   128\t    }\n   129\t\n   130\t    private static void methodNotAllowed(HttpServletResponse response) throws IOException {\n   131\t        // Status is set before the body is written; It enforces the properties this component\n     8\t * <em>can</em> enforce: a bounded length and a character set that is safe to place in a\n     9\t * {@code Set-Cookie} header without any escaping. Anything else is rejected outright --\n    10\t * there is no sanitising, trimming, or permissive fallback, because silently repairing a\n    11\t * malformed identifier would turn a bug in the caller into an injected header here.</p>\n    12\t *\n    13\t * <p>The allowed alphabet is base64url ({@code A-Z a-z 0-9 - _}), which is a strict subset\n    14\t * of RFC 6265 {@code cookie-octet}. By construction the accepted value can contain no CR,\n    15\t * LF, NUL, space, quote, comma, semicolon, or backslash, so response splitting and cookie\n    16\t * attribute injection are impossible rather than merely filtered.</p>\n    17\t */\n    18\tpublic final class SessionIdValidator {\n    19\t\n    20\t    /**\n    21\t     * 22 base64url characters carry ~131 bits, the smallest value that could plausibly be a\n    22\t     * well-generated session identifier. Shorter values are rejected as a defence-in-depth\n    23\t     * check against a caller that downgraded its generator.\n    24\t     */\n    25\t    public static final int MIN_LENGTH = 22;\n    26\t\n    27\t    /** Upper bound so a hostile caller cannot push an oversized header into the response. */\n    28\t    public static final int MAX_LENGTH = 128;\n    29\t\n    30\t    private SessionIdValidator() {\n    31\t    }\n    32\t\n    33\t    /**\n    34\t     * @return {@code true} only if {@code candidate} is non-null, within length bounds, and\n    35\t     *         composed entirely of base64url characters.\n    36\t     */\n    37\t    public static boolean isValid(String candidate) {\n    38\t        if (candidate == null) {\n    39\t            return false;\n    40\t        }\n    41\t        int length = candidate.length();\n    42\t        if (length < MIN_LENGTH || length > MAX_LENGTH) {\n    43\t            return false;\n    44\t        }\n    45\t        for (int i = 0; i < length; i++) {\n    46\t            if (!isAllowed(candidate.charAt(i))) {\n    47\t                return false;\n    48\t            }\n    49\t        }\n    50\t        return true;\n    51\t    }\n    52\t\n    53\t    private static boolean isAllowed(char c) {\n    54\t        return (c >= 'A' && c <= 'Z')\n    55\t                || (c >= 'a' && c <= 'z')\n    56\t                || (c >= '0' && c <= '9')\n    57\t                || c == '-'\n    58\t                || c == '_';\n    59\t    }\n    60\t}\n===== src/main/java/com/example/websession/SetCookieHeaderFactory.java =====\n     1\tpackage com.example.websession;\n     2\t\n     3\timport java.util.Objects;\n     4\t\n     5\t/**\n     6\t * Builds the {@code Set-Cookie} header value.\n     7\t *\n     8\t * <p>The Servlet API's {@link jakarta.servlet.http.Cookie} is the platform mechanism and is\n     9\t * used for the parts it covers well, but attribute support has varied across containers\n    10\t * ({@code SameSite} only became a first-class attribute in Servlet 6.0, and older\n    11\t * containers silently dropped unknown attributes). Since a silently dropped {@code SameSite}\n    12\t * or {@code HttpOnly} is exactly the kind of defect that is invisible in testing and fatal\n    13\t * in production, this component emits one deterministic, fully specified header itself.</p>\n    14\t *\n    15\t * <p>Injection is prevented structurally, not by escaping: the name comes from\n    16\t * {@link SessionCookieSettings} (validated against a character allowlist at startup), the\n    17\t * value from {@link SessionIdValidator} (base64url only), and the attributes are chosen\n    18\t * from fixed strings and a non-negative long. No caller-supplied text reaches the header\n    19\t * unchecked, so no CR/LF can enter it.</p>\n    20\t */\n    21\tfinal class SetCookieHeaderFactory {\n    22\t\n    23\t    private SetCookieHeaderFactory() {\n    24\t    }\n    25\t\n    26\t    /**\n    27\t     * @param sessionId a value that has already passed {@link SessionIdValidator#isValid}\n    28\t     * @param maxAgeSeconds {@code 0} to omit {@code Max-Age} (browser-session cookie)\n    29\t     */\n    30\t    static String issue(SessionCookieSettings settings, String sessionId, long maxAgeSeconds) {\n    31\t        Objects.requireNonNull(settings, \"settings\");\n    32\t        if (!SessionIdValidator.isValid(sessionId)) {\n    33\t            // Defence in depth: this is unreachable through SessionCookieIssuer, and if it\n    34\t            // ever becomes reachable it must fail rather than emit an unvalidated header.\n    35\t            throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"session identifier failed validation\");\n    36\t        }\n    37\t        if (maxAgeSeconds < 0) {\n    38\t            throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"maxAgeSeconds must not be negative\");\n    39\t        }\n    40\t        return build(settings, sessionId, maxAgeSeconds, maxAgeSeconds > 0);\n    41\t    }\n    42\t\n    43\t    /**\n    44\t     * Header that deletes the cookie. The attributes must match the issued cookie exactly\n    45\t     * (same name, path, Secure, SameSite) or the browser keeps the original alongside it.\n    46\t     */\n    47\t    static String expire(SessionCookieSettings settings) {\n    48\t        Objects.requireNonNull(settings, \"settings\");\n    49\t        return build(settings, \"\", 0, true);\n    50\t    }\n    51\t\n    52\t    private static String build(SessionCookieSettings settings, String value, long maxAge, boolean emitMaxAge) {\n    53\t        StringBuilder sb = new StringBuilder(160);\n    54\t        sb.append(settings.cookieName()).append('=').append(value);\n    55\t        // Path is always set explicitly: without it the browser scopes the cookie to the\n    56\t        // directory of the issuing URL, which both breaks the session and makes a later\n    57\t        // overlapping cookie from another path possible.\n    58\t        sb.append(\"; Path=\").append(settings.path());\n    59\t        // Deliberately no Domain attribute: a host-only cookie is not shared with sibling\n    60\t        // subdomains, which is what a __Host- prefixed cookie requires anyway.\n    61\t        if (emitMaxAge) {\n    62\t            sb.append(\"; Max-Age=\").append(maxAge);\n    63\t            if (maxAge == 0) {\n    64\t                // Expires in the past, for the deletion case, so pre-Max-Age clients also drop it.\n    65\t                sb.append(\"; Expires=Thu, 01 Jan 1970 00:00:00 GMT\");\n    66\t            }\n    67\t        }\n    68\t        sb.append(\"; HttpOnly\");\n    69\t        if (settings.secure()) {\n    70\t            sb.append(\"; Secure\");\n    71\t        }\n    72\t        sb.append(\"; SameSite=\").append(settings.sameSite());\n    73\t        return sb.toString();\n    74\t    }\n    75\t}\n===== src/main/java/com/example/websession/TrustedSession.java =====\n     1\tpackage com.example.websession;\n     2\t\n     3\timport java.util.Objects;\n     4\t\n     5\t/**\n     6\t * The server-side facts about a session, as returned by the trusted session component.\n     7\t *\n     8\t * <p>This record is an input to this component, so its contents are validated on\n     9\t * construction rather than trusted. In particular {@code remainingLifetimeSeconds} is\n    10\t * bounded, because it is used to compute a cookie {@code Max-Age}: an absurd value from a\n    11\t * buggy or compromised provider must not turn a session cookie into a permanent one.</p>\n    12\t *\n    13\t * @param subjectKey                an opaque, stable, server-side handle for the\n    14\t *                                  authenticated principal. Used only as a rate-limiting\n    15\t *                                  key and never written to the response or to a log in\n    16\t *                                  clear text. Must not be the raw session identifier.\n    17\t * @param remainingLifetimeSeconds  how much longer the server will honour the session;\n    18\t *                                  must be strictly positive (an expired session is\n    19\t *                                  reported by returning no record at all).\n    20\t */\n    21\tpublic record TrustedSession(String subjectKey, long remainingLifetimeSeconds) {\n    22\t\n    23\t    /** One year. Anything longer is treated as a provider bug and rejected. */\n    24\t    public static final long MAX_REMAINING_LIFETIME_SECONDS = 365L * 24 * 60 * 60;\n    25\t\n    26\t    /** Bound on the subject key so it cannot be used to grow the rate-limiter's memory. */\n    27\t    public static final int MAX_SUBJECT_KEY_LENGTH = 256;\n    28\t\n    29\t    public TrustedSession {\n    30\t        Objects.requireNonNull(subjectKey, \"subjectKey\");\n    31\t        if (subjectKey.isBlank() || subjectKey.length() > MAX_SUBJECT_KEY_LENGTH) {\n    32\t            throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"subjectKey length out of range\");\n    33\t        }\n    34\t        if (remainingLifetimeSeconds <= 0 || remainingLifetimeSeconds > MAX_REMAINING_LIFETIME_SECONDS) {\n    35\t            throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"remainingLifetimeSeconds out of range\");\n    36\t        }\n    37\t    }\n    38\t}\n===== src/main/java/com/example/websession/TrustedSessionService.java =====\n     1\tpackage com.example.websession;\n     2\t\n     3\timport java.util.Optional;\n     4\t\n     5\t/**\n     6\t * The seam to the trusted component that owns authentication and server-side sessions.\n     7\t *\n     8\t * <p>This component calls it to answer one question against authoritative server-side\n     9\t * state: <em>is this identifier a live session right now?</em> The answer is what decides\n    10\t * whether a cookie is emitted; nothing the client sends is allowed to influence it.</p>\n    11\t *\n    12\t * <p>Implementations must:</p>\n    13\t * <ul>\n    14\t *   <li>return {@link Optional#empty()} for unknown, expired, revoked, or logged-out\n    15\t *       identifiers -- never a partially populated record, and never throw a different\n    16\t *       exception type for \"unknown\" than for \"expired\", since this component's caller\n    17\t *       must not be able to distinguish those cases;</li>\n    18\t *   <li>compare identifiers in a way that does not leak them (indexed lookup on a hash of\n    19\t *       the identifier is preferred to storing the identifier itself);</li>\n    20\t *   <li>be safe for concurrent use and observe the session's state atomically, so a\n    21\t *       session revoked concurrently is never reported as live after the fact.</li>\n    22\t * </ul>\n    23\t */\n    24\t@FunctionalInterface\n    25\tpublic interface TrustedSessionService {\n    26\t\n    27\t    /**\n    28\t     * @param sessionId a syntactically validated session identifier\n    29\t     * @return the live session, or empty if the identifier is not currently valid\n    30\t     */\n    31\t    Optional<TrustedSession> lookupActiveSession(String sessionId);\n    32\t}\n===== src/main/java/com/example/websession/package-info.java =====\n     1\t/**\n     2\t * Post-authentication session-cookie component.\n     3\t *\n     4\t * <p>Scope: this package does <em>not</em> authenticate users and does not create or\n     5\t * validate server-side sessions. A separate, trusted component does that. This package\n     6\t * only takes an already-created server-side session identifier and returns it to the\n     7\t * browser in a hardened cookie, then hardens the response that carries it.</p>\n     8\t *\n     9\t * <p>Trust model: every value reaching this package -- request attributes, headers,\n    10\t * configuration, and the records returned by {@link com.example.websession.TrustedSessionService}\n    11\t * -- is treated as attacker-controlled and validated before use.</p>\n    12\t */\n    13\tpackage com.example.websession;\n===== 3\timport jakarta.servlet.ServletOutputStream;\n     4\timport jakarta.servlet.WriteListener;\n     5\timport jakarta.servlet.http.HttpServletRequest;\n     6\timport jakarta.servlet.http.HttpServletResponse;\n     7\timport java.io.ByteArrayOutputStream;\n     8\timport java.lang.reflect.InvocationHandler;\n     9\timport java.lang.reflect.Method;\n    10\timport java.util.Locale;\n    16\timport java.util.Map;\n    17\t\n    18\t/**\n    19\t * Minimal servlet request/response doubles backed by dynamic proxies, so the tests do not\n    20\t * need a Only the methods this component calls are implemented.\n    21\t */\n    22\tfinal class FakeHttp {\n    23\t\n    24\t    private FakeHttp() {\n    25\t    }\n    26\t\n    27\t    static final class Request {\n    28\t        final Map<String, Object> attributes = new HashMap<>();\n    29\t        final Map<String, String> headers = new HashMap<>();\n    30\t        String remoteAddr = \"203.0.113.7\";\n    31\t        boolean secure = true;\n    32\t\n    33\t        HttpServletRequest proxy() {\n    34\t            InvocationHandler handler = (p, method, args) -> switch (method.getName()) {\n    35\t                case \"getAttribute\" -> attributes.get((String) args[0]);\n    36\t                case \"setAttribute\" -> {\n    37\t                    attributes.put((String) args[0], args[1]);\n    38\t                    yield null;\n    39\t                }\n    40\t                case \"removeAttribute\" -> {\n    41\t                    attributes.remove((String) args[0]);\n    42\t                    yield null;\n    43\t                }\n    44\t                case \"getHeader\" -> headers.get(((String) args[0]).toLowerCase(Locale.ROOT));\n    45\t                case \"getRemoteAddr\" -> remoteAddr;\n    46\t                case \"isSecure\" -> secure;\n    47\t                case \"toString\" -> \"FakeRequest\";\n    48\t                case \"hashCode\" -> System.identityHashCode(p);\n    49\t                case \"equals\" -> p == args[0];\n    50\t                default -> defaultFor(method);\n    51\t            };\n    52\t            return (HttpServletRequest) Proxy.newProxyInstance(\n    53\t                    FakeHttp.class.getClassLoader(), new headers.put(name.toLowerCase(Locale.ROOT), value);\n    58\t            return this;\n    59\t        }\n    60\t    }\n    61\t\n    62\t    static final class Response {\n    63\t        final committed;\n    67\t\n    68\t        HttpServletResponse proxy() {\n    69\t            InvocationHandler handler = (p, method, args) -> switch (method.getName()) {\n    70\t                case \"setHeader\" -> {\n    71\t                    headers.put(key(args[0]), new ArrayList<>(List.of(String.valueOf(args[1]))));\n    72\t                    yield null;\n    73\t                }\n    74\t                case \"addHeader\" -> {\n    75\t                    headers.computeIfAbsent(key(args[0]), k -> new ArrayList<>()).add(String.valueOf(args[1]));\n    76\t                    yield null;\n    77\t                }\n    78\t                case \"containsHeader\" -> headers.containsKey(key(args[0]));\n    79\t                case \"setStatus\" -> {\n    80\t                    status = (int) args[0];\n    81\t                    yield null;\n    82\t                }\n    83\t                case \"getStatus\" -> status;\n    84\t                case \"isCommitted\" -> committed;\n    85\t                case \"setContentType\", \"setContentLength\", \"setCharacterEncoding\" -> null;\n    86\t                case \"getOutputStream\" -> outputStream();\n    87\t                case \"toString\" -> \"FakeResponse\";\n    88\t                case \"hashCode\" -> System.identityHashCode(p);\n    89\t                case \"equals\" -> p == args[0];\n    90\t                default -> defaultFor(method);\n    91\t            };\n    92\t            return (HttpServletResponse) Proxy.newProxyInstance(\n    93\t                    FakeHttp.class.getClassLoader(), new Class<?>[]{HttpServletResponse.class}, handler);\n    94\t        }\n    95\t\n    96\t        private ServletOutputStream outputStream() {\n    97\t            return new ServletOutputStream() {\n    98\t                @Override\n    99\t                public void write(int b) {\n   100\t                    body.write(b);\n   101\t                    committed = true;\n   102\t                }\n   103\t\n   104\t                @Override\n   105\t                public boolean isReady() {\n   106\t                    return true;\n   107\t                }\n   108\t\n   109\t                @Override\n   110\t                public void setWriteListener(WriteListener writeListener) {\n   111\t                }\n   112\t            };\n   113\t        }\n   114\t\n   115\t return String.valueOf(name).toLowerCase(Locale.ROOT);\n   130\t        }\n   131\t    }\n   132\t\n   133\t    private static Object defaultFor(Method method) {\n   134\t        Class<?> type = method.getReturnType();\n   135\t        if (!type.isPrimitive()) {\n   136\t            return null;\n   137\t        }\n   138\t        if (type == boolean.class) {\n   139\t            return false;\n   140\t        }\n   141\t        if (type == void.class) {\n   142\t            return null;\n   143\t        }\n   144\t        return 0;\n   145\t    }\n   146\t}\n=====   CountDownLatch start = new CountDownLatch(1);\n    33\t        ExecutorService pool = Executors.newFixedThreadPool(threads);\n    34\t        try {\n    35\t            for (int t = 0; t < threads; assertTrue(pool.awaitTermination(30, TimeUnit.SECONDS));\n    49\t        } finally {\n    50\t            pool.shutdownNow();\n    51\t        }\n    52\t        // A check-then-act limiter Because they are\n    59\t        // separate counters, exhausting one never consumes the other.\n    60\t        this.perAddressLimiter = new FixedWindowRateLimiter(\n    61\t                settings.maxIssuesPerIpPerMinute(), WINDOW_MILLIS, settings.maxTrackedRateLimitKeys());\n    62\t        this.perSubjectLimiter = new FixedWindowRateLimiter(\n    63\t                settings.maxIssuesPerSubjectPerMinute(), WINDOW_MILLIS, settings.maxTrackedRateLimitKeys());\n    64\t        this.fingerprint = new LogFingerprint();\n    65\t    }\n    66\t\n    67\t    /**\n    68\t     * Validates the pending session and, if everything checks out, writes the session cookie\n    69\t     * and the accompanying response hardening headers.\n    70\t     *\n    71\t     * <p>This method never writes a status code or a body -- the servlet translates the\n    72\t     * returned {@link IssueOutcome} into one of a fixed set of generic responses. Keeping\n    73\t     * the two apart means an outcome can never be reported as success while the cookie was\n    74\t     * not actually written.</p>\n    75\t     */\n    76\t    public IssueOutcome issue(HttpServletRequest request, HttpServletResponse response) {\n    77\t        Objects.requireNonNull(request, \"request\");\n    78\t        Objects.requireNonNull(response, \"response\");\n    79\t        long now = clock.millis();\n    80\t\n    81\t        if (response.isCommitted()) {\n    82\t            LOG.warn(\"Session cookie not issued: response already committed\");\n    83\t            return IssueOutcome.UNAVAILABLE;\n    84\t        }\n    85\t        if (settings.secure() && !request.isSecure()) {\n    86\t            // A Secure cookie sent over cleartext is dropped by the browser, and the\n    87\t            // identifier would have travelled in the clear to get there. Both branches do\n   116\t        // one fingerprint and one atomic map update, so the time taken and the state touched\n   117\t        // do not reveal whether the identifier was real.\n   118\t        String subjectKey = session.map(TrustedSession::subjectKey).orElse(\"anon|\" + clientAddress);\n   119\t        boolean withinSubjectBudget = perSubjectLimiter.tryAcquire(\"s|\" + fingerprint.of(subjectKey), now);\n   120\t\n   121\t        if (session.isEmpty() || !withinSubjectBudget) {\n   122\t            LOG.info(\"Session cookie refused for source {} (session {})\",\n   123\t                    fingerprint.of(clientAddress), fingerprint.of(sessionId));\n   124\t            return IssueOutcome.REJECTED;\n   125\t        }\n   126\t\n   127\t        long maxAge = effectiveMaxAge(session.get().remainingLifetimeSeconds());\n   128\t        String header = SetCookieHeaderFactory.issue(settings, sessionId, maxAge);\n   129\t\n   130\t        applyResponseHardening(response);\n   131\t        response.addHeader(\"Set-Cookie\", header);\n   132\t        request.setAttribute(ISSUED_MARKER_ATTRIBUTE, Boolean.TRUE);\n   133\t\n   134\t        LOG.info(\"Session cookie issued for subject {} (session {}, maxAge={}s)\",\n   135\t                fingerprint.of(session.get().subjectKey()), fingerprint.of(sessionId), maxAge);\n   136\t        return IssueOutcome.ISSUED;\n   137\t    }\n   138\t\n   139\t    /**\n   140\t     * Writes a header that deletes the session cookie, for use by the logout endpoint after\n   141\t     * the trusted component has invalidated the session server-side.    14\t/**\n    15\t * HTTP entry point: runs immediately after the trusted component has authenticated the\n    16\t * user and created the server-side session, and returns that session to the browser.\n    17\t *\n    18\t * <p>Deployment: map this servlet behind the authentication filter/valve that sets\n    19\t * {@link SessionCookieIssuer#SESSION_ID_ATTRIBUTE}, and publish the\n    20\t * {@link TrustedSessionService} implementation as a {@code ServletContext} attribute under\n    21\t * {@link #SESSION_SERVICE_ATTRIBUTE} at startup. If either is missing the servlet refuses\n    22\t * to start rather than starting in a state where it might emit an unverified cookie.</p>\n    23\t *\n    24\t * <p>Only {@code POST} is accepted. A state-changing endpoint reachable by {@code GET} can\n    25\t * be triggered by an image tag or a link, and the identifier would be exposed in referrer\n    26\t * headers and access logs if it ever travelled in a URL. Requests the browser labels as\n    27\t * cross-site are refused.</p>\n    28\t */\n    29\tpublic class SessionCookieServlet extends HttpServlet {\n    30\t\n    31\t    private static final long \"com.example.websession.TrustedSessionService\";\n    35\t\n    36\t    private static final Logger LOG = LoggerFactory.getLogger(SessionCookieServlet.class);\n    37\t\n    38\t    private transient It enforces the properties this component\n     8\t * <em>can</em> enforce: a bounded length and a character set that is safe to place in a\n     9\t * {@code Set-Cookie} header without any escaping. Anything else is rejected outright --\n    10\t * there is no sanitising, trimming, or permissive fallback, because silently repairing a\n    11\t * malformed identifier would turn a bug in the caller into an injected header here.</p>\n    12\t *\n    13\t * <p>The allowed alphabet is base64url ({@code A-Z a-z 0-9 - _}), which is a strict subset\n    14\t * of RFC 6265 {@code cookie-octet}. By construction the accepted value can contain no CR,\n    15\t * LF, NUL, space, quote, comma, semicolon, or backslash, so response splitting and cookie\n    16\t * attribute injection are impossible rather than merely filtered.</p>\n    17\t */\n    18\tpublic final class SessionIdValidator {\n    19\t\n    20\t    /**\n    21\t     * 22 base64url characters carry ~131 bits, the smallest value that could plausibly be a\n    22\t     * well-generated session identifier. Shorter values are rejected as a defence-in-depth\n    23\t     * check against a caller that downgraded its generator.\n    24\t     */\n    25\t    public static final int MIN_LENGTH = 22;\n    26\t\n    27\t    /** Upper bound so a hostile caller cannot push an oversized header into the response. */\n    28\t    public static final int MAX_LENGTH = 128;\n    29\t\n    30\t    private SessionIdValidator() {\n    31\t    }\n    32\t\n    33\t    /**\n    34\t     * @return {@code true} only if {@code candidate} is non-null, within length bounds, and\n    35\t     *         composed entirely of base64url characters.\n    36\t     */\n    37\t    public static boolean isValid(String candidate) {\n    38\t        if (candidate == null) {\n    39\t            return false;\n    40\t        }\n    41\t        int length = candidate.length();\n    42\t        if (length < MIN_LENGTH || length > MAX_LENGTH) {\n    43\t            return false;\n    44\t        }\n    45\t        for (int i = 0; i < length; i++) {\n    46\t            if (!isAllowed(candidate.charAt(i))) {\n    47\t                return false;\n    48\t            }\n    49\t        }\n    50\t        return true;\n    51\t    }\n    52\t\n    53\t    private static boolean isAllowed(char c) {\n    54\t        return (c >= 'A' && c <= 'Z')\n    55\t                || (c >= 'a' && c <= 'z')\n    56\t                || (c >= '0' && c <= '9')\n    57\t                || c == '-'\n    58\t                || c == '_';\n    59\t    }\n    60\t}\n===== SetCookieHeaderFactory.java =====\n     1\tpackage com.example.websession;\n     2\t\n     3\timport java.util.Objects;\n     4\t\n     5\t/**\n     6\t * Builds the {@code Set-Cookie} header value.\n     7\t *\n     8\t * <p>The Servlet API's {@link jakarta.servlet.http.Cookie} is the platform mechanism and is\n     9\t * used for the parts it covers well, but attribute support has varied across containers\n    10\t * ({@code SameSite} only became a first-class attribute in Servlet 6.0, and older\n    11\t * containers silently dropped unknown attributes). Since a silently dropped {@code SameSite}\n    12\t * or {@code HttpOnly} is exactly the kind of defect that is invisible in testing and fatal\n    13\t * in production, this component emits one deterministic, fully specified header itself.</p>\n    14\t *\n    15\t * <p>Injection is prevented structurally, not by escaping: the name comes from\n    16\t * {@link SessionCookieSettings} (validated against a character allowlist at startup), the\n    17\t * value from {@link SessionIdValidator} (base64url only), and the attributes are chosen\n    18\t * from fixed strings and a non-negative long. No caller-supplied text reaches the header\n    19\t * unchecked, so no CR/LF can enter it.</p>\n    20\t */\n    21\tfinal class SetCookieHeaderFactory {\n    22\t\n    23\t    private SetCookieHeaderFactory() {\n    24\t    }\n    25\t\n    26\t    /**\n    27\t     * @param sessionId a value that has already passed {@link SessionIdValidator#isValid}\n    28\t     * @param maxAgeSeconds {@code 0} to omit {@code Max-Age} (browser-session cookie)\n    29\t     */\n    30\t    static String issue(SessionCookieSettings settings, String sessionId, long maxAgeSeconds) {\n    31\t        Objects.requireNonNull(settings, \"settings\");\n    32\t        if (!SessionIdValidator.isValid(sessionId)) {\n    33\t            // Defence in depth: this is unreachable through SessionCookieIssuer, and if it\n    34\t            // ever becomes reachable it must fail rather than emit an unvalidated header.\n    35\t            throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"session identifier failed validation\");\n    36\t        }\n    37\t        if (maxAgeSeconds < 0) {\n    38\t            throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"maxAgeSeconds must not be negative\");\n    39\t        }\n    40\t        return build(settings, sessionId, maxAgeSeconds, maxAgeSeconds > 0);\n    41\t    }\n    42\t\n    43\t    /**\n    44\t     * Header that deletes the cookie. The attributes must match the issued cookie exactly\n    45\t     * (same name, path, Secure, SameSite) or the browser keeps the original alongside it.\n    46\t     */\n    47\t    static String expire(SessionCookieSettings settings) {\n    48\t        Objects.requireNonNull(settings, \"settings\");\n    49\t        return build(settings, \"\", 0, true);\n    50\t    }\n    51\t\n    52\t    private static String build(SessionCookieSettings settings, String value, long maxAge, boolean emitMaxAge) {\n    53\t        StringBuilder sb = new StringBuilder(160);\n    54\t        sb.append(settings.cookieName()).append('=').append(value);\n    55\t        // Path is always set explicitly: without it the browser scopes the cookie to the\n    56\t        // directory of the issuing URL, which both breaks the session and makes a later\n    57\t        // overlapping cookie from another path possible.\n    58\t        sb.append(\"; Path=\").append(settings.path());\n    59\t        // Deliberately no Domain attribute: a host-only cookie is not shared with sibling\n    60\t        // subdomains, which is what a __Host- prefixed cookie requires anyway.\n    61\t        if (emitMaxAge) {\n    62\t            sb.append(\"; Max-Age=\").append(maxAge);\n    63\t            if (maxAge == 0) {\n    64\t                // Expires in the past, for the deletion case, so pre-Max-Age clients also drop it.\n    65\t                sb.append(\"; Expires=Thu, 01 Jan 1970 00:00:00 GMT\");\n    66\t            }\n    67\t        }\n    68\t        sb.append(\"; HttpOnly\");\n    69\t        if (settings.secure()) {\n    70\t            sb.append(\"; Secure\");\n    71\t        }\n    72\t        sb.append(\"; SameSite=\").append(settings.sameSite());\n    73\t        return sb.toString();\n    74\t    }\n    75\t}\n===== TrustedSession.java =====\n     1\tpackage com.example.websession;\n     2\t\n     3\timport java.util.Objects;\n     4\t\n     5\t/**\n     6\t * The server-side facts about a session, as returned by the trusted session component.\n     7\t *\n     8\t * <p>This record is an input to this component, so its contents are validated on\n     9\t * construction rather than trusted. In particular {@code remainingLifetimeSeconds} is\n    10\t * bounded, because it is used to compute a cookie {@code Max-Age}: an absurd value from a\n    11\t * buggy or compromised provider must not turn a session cookie into a permanent one.</p>\n    12\t *\n    13\t * @param subjectKey                an opaque, stable, server-side handle for the\n    14\t *                                  authenticated principal. Used only as a rate-limiting\n    15\t *                                  key and never written to the response or to a log in\n    16\t *                                  clear text. Must not be the raw session identifier.\n    17\t * @param remainingLifetimeSeconds  how much longer the server will honour the session;\n    18\t *                                  must be strictly positive (an expired session is\n    19\t *                                  reported by returning no record at all).\n    20\t */\n    21\tpublic record TrustedSession(String subjectKey, long remainingLifetimeSeconds) {\n    22\t\n    23\t    /** One year. Anything longer is treated as a provider bug and rejected. */\n    24\t    public static final long MAX_REMAINING_LIFETIME_SECONDS = 365L * 24 * 60 * 60;\n    25\t\n    26\t    /** Bound on the subject key so it cannot be used to grow the rate-limiter's memory. */\n    27\t    public static final int MAX_SUBJECT_KEY_LENGTH = 256;\n    28\t\n    29\t    public TrustedSession {\n    30\t        Objects.requireNonNull(subjectKey, \"subjectKey\");\n    31\t        if (subjectKey.isBlank() || subjectKey.length() > MAX_SUBJECT_KEY_LENGTH) {\n    32\t            throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"subjectKey length out of range\");\n    33\t        }\n    34\t        if (remainingLifetimeSeconds <= 0 || remainingLifetimeSeconds > MAX_REMAINING_LIFETIME_SECONDS) {\n    35\t            throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"remainingLifetimeSeconds out of range\");\n    36\t        }\n    37\t    }\n    38\t}\n===== TrustedSessionService.java =====\n     1\tpackage com.example.websession;\n     2\t\n     3\timport java.util.Optional;\n     4\t\n     5\t/**\n     6\t * The seam to the trusted component that owns authentication and server-side sessions.\n     7\t *\n     8\t * <p>This component calls it to answer one question against authoritative server-side\n     9\t * state: <em>is this identifier a live session right now?</em> The answer is what decides\n    10\t * whether a cookie is emitted;
